Let's detail each helper.

Fast convolution:
```
def conv_dot(a): # a 1D float n
  n=len(a); M=2*n-1; L=1 << (M-1).bit_length(); A=np.fft.rfft(a,L); c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:M]; return c
```
Score:
```
def score_of(a):
  c=conv_dot(a); S=a.sum(); if S<... return inf; return 2*n*c.max()/(S*S)
```
Normalize: `norm1(a)` returns a/a.sum() if sum>0.

LSE value/grad:
```
def lse_smooth_value_grad(a, beta):
   n=len(a); M=2n-1; L=fft_len
   c=conv_dot(a); S=sum
   cm = c.max()
   # For stability if beta*big? c nonnegative normalized sum=1; max maybe ~0.0002; beta up to 10000 product ~2 no issue? Actually n 8192, max ~1/(2n)=6e-5, beta*6e-5 small if beta 10000 ->0.6. beta can be 1e6 ->60. choose shift.
   scaled=beta*(c-cm)
   ex=np.exp(scaled); Z=sum; if under? ex max 1.
   Lval=cm+np.log(Z)/beta
   p=ex/Z
   # g_i = sum_j p_{j+i} x_j
   L2=1 << ((M+n-1)-1).bit_length()? conv length M+n-1=3n-2. Need FFT len >=3n-1.
   # We can compute via FFT or use np.convolve? n=8192, p dense; np.convolve O(n^2) too slow. Use FFT.
   p_rev = p[::-1]
   Lc = 1 << ((M+n-1)-1).bit_length()
   X=np.fft.rfft(a,Lc); PR=np.fft.rfft(p_rev,Lc); conv=np.fft.irfft(X*PR,Lc)
   # g_i = conv[M-1-i] for i=0..n-1. M-1=2n-2.
   g = conv[2*n-2 - np.arange(n)]  # this creates not allowed? np.arange top-level ok inside helper fine.
   grad = (4.0*n)*g/(S*S) - (4.0*n)*Lval/(S**3)
   # Optionally remove mean to handle scaling:
   grad = grad - np.mean(grad)
   return Lval? score, grad
```

Implement `smooth_score_grad_normalized(x, beta)`:
```
S=x.sum(); if S<...: return large, zeros
b=x/S
c=conv_dot(b)
cm=c.max()
if beta small: scaled=beta*(c-cm)
Z=sum(exp)
L=cm+log(Z)/beta
F=2n L
# p
p=exp/Z
# compute g via conv
Lc=1 << ((M+n-1)-1).bit_length()
X=rfft(b,Lc); PR=rfft(p[::-1],Lc); conv=irfft(X*PR,Lc)
g=conv[2*n-2 - arange(n)]
H=4*n*(g - np.mean(g))
# optional clamp grad
grad=H/S
return F, grad
```

Let's define helper functions concretely.

`fft_len_for_linear(n, factor=1?)`: for convolution length M=2n-1 -> next pow2 >=M. For cross gradient length 3n-2 -> next pow2.

`convolution(a)`:
```
def convolution(a):
    n = a.size
    M = 2*n -1
    L = 1 << ((M-1).bit_length())
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:M]
    c[np.abs(c)<1e-18]=0
    return c
```

`score(a)`:
```
def score(a):
  a=np.asarray(a,float)
  S=a.sum()
  if S<1e-12: return np.inf
  c=convolution(a)
  m=c.max()
  if m<0: m=0
  return float(2.0*a.size*m)/(S*S)
```

`normalize(a)`:
```
def normalize(a):
  a=np.asarray(a,float)
  S=a.sum()
  if S<=1e-12: return np.ones_like(a)
  return a/S
```

For n=8192, P=12 => ~200k memory okay. But multiple lengths. We can create on fly? Better helper `fourier_seq(n, coeff, P)`:
```
x=np.arange(n)/n
seq=np.ones(n)*coeff[0]
for m in range(1,P+1):
  w=2π*m*x
  seq += coeff[m*?]*cos + ...
```
For low-dim optimization, computing trig each eval O(nP) ~8192*20=164k, okay. But could precompute basis.

Another novel: **active set reflective method with polynomial square root**.
Let's implement a function `sqrt_root_reflection(a, n_iters=200, alpha=0.5)`:
- Normalize a.
- For it:
   c=conv(a)
   # target c: set coefficients too high down to current max? We want flatten: target = c.copy(); target[target > D] = D, where D maybe max * (1 - something) or median? But if just clip high, sqrt inverse may reduce high.
   D = c.max()
   target = np.minimum(c, D * 0.99?) maybe lower all? This tends to reduce.
   # Add small floor to avoid negative DFT sqrt: C=rfft(target,L). Its values may have negative real? sqrt complex fine.
   C = np.fft.rfft(target,L)
   A = np.sqrt(C + 0j)
   # Enforce Hermitian: for k=1..L/2, A[L-k]=conj(A[k]) but rfft stores half; irfft assumes. But we may need choose signs to improve nonnegative/time support.
   At iterate, generate random sign flips: `s = np.ones(len(A)); s[1:] = np.where(rng.random(len(A)-1)<0.05, -1, 1); A = A*s`? Flipping sign changes A^2? sign^2=1, so C unchanged. Inverse time changes. This is a global move preserving target.
   a_t = np.fft.irfft(A,L)[:n]
   # Project to first n support: take part = a_t[:n]; if sum small maybe take abs? Use a_new = max(part,0)
   mix = 0.5
   a = normalize(np.maximum((1-mix)*a + mix*a_new,0))
- Track best exact.
This may produce diverse sequences. But target sqrt with random signs may produce a_t with large values in tail; taking first n may lose mass. Maybe if target is from current a, signs near current yield small tail? Current A = rfft(a) is one root; random small sign flips stay near. Could be okay.

There isn't much free except signs (phase π) as above. Could also choose a different root per bin continuously? For real c, C_k has argument θ; roots have arguments θ/2 and θ/2+π. Only two choices. But sqrt of target c arbitrary. If target is very flat (constant avg), C has only DC? Wait target length M=2n-1 constant -> rfft C: only DC if length exactly M? For L power two, C_k not exactly zero because target padded with zeros after M to L? If target length M constant for 0..M-1 and zeros after to L, DFT has many components. A=sqrt(C), inverse time. Another method: **direct enforced convolution via polynomial Newton**. We can choose target full c (length 2n-1) with many degrees, solve for a by truncating first n square root. In polynomial algebra modulo z^{2n-1}, we need A(z)^2 ≡ C(z) mod z^{2n-1}. If C has constant >0, there are solutions A of degree up to 2n-2; we only take first n coefficients. But the full C includes right coefficients; consistency. We can optimize C (nonnegative sum1, maxD) and set A=left n coefficients of sqrt(C). This is same as left profile but now C length 2n. Could use iterative: given a, set C=conv(a); define improved C' by replacing each coefficient with target (e.g., clipping to D, adding floor). Compute A'=sqrt(C') full (Newton to length 2n-1), take first n coefficients. This may enforce both left and right targets simultaneously. ```
def poly_sqrt(C, a_init, M):
  # C length M, a_init length M with a_init[0]>0
  A=a_init.copy()
  # ensure A[0]=sqrt(C[0]) maybe A[0]=sqrt(C[0])
  # Newton doubling:
  m=1
  while m<M:
     m2=min(2*m,M)
     # compute C/A mod z^m2 using convolution
     # Use FFT length 2*m2
     L=1 << ((2*m2-1)-1).bit_length()
     Ai=rfft(A[:m],L); Ci=rfft(C[:m2],L)
     Q=irfft(Ci/Ai,L)[:m2]
     A = 0.5*(A + Q)  # but A length m, need pad to m2 for first M? Actually A has m coefficients; Q has m2. The formula mod z^m2 uses old A (m coeffs) as denominator approximating up to m. Q gives first m2 terms. Set A = (A_old_padded + Q)/2.
     A = A[:m2]
     m=m2
  return A
```

- H=4*n*(g - np.mean(g))
- grad=H/S

Implement `exact_score` carefully for list input:
```
def exact_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    S=a.sum()
    if S<1e-12: return np.inf
    c=linear_conv(a)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    if m<0: m=0.0
    n=a.size
    return float(2.0*n*m)/(S*S)
```
Scale invariant. If a is integer type ok.

`normalize` returns sum1.

`b_sequence(n)`:
```
def b_sequence(n):
    b=np.empty(n)
    b[0]=1.0
    for i in range(1,n): b[i]=b[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
    return normalize(b)
```
This is pure left power p=0.5. For large n, recurrence stable.

`power_seq(n,p, eps=1e-6)`:
```
i=np.arange(n)
a=(i+1+eps)^(-p)
# maybe add right small? normalize
```
Add variants p=0.5,0.3.

`two_level_seq(n,p_left)`: Half? continuous two-level optimal p=1/3? For one-sided: left half density 2p? Let's generate `a[:n//2]= (2*p)/n`? For p=1/3 high? Need nonnegative. Simpler: `a = np.ones(n); a[:n//2]=r; a[n//2:]=1; normalize`. Sweep r 0.1,1, multi. But not important.

`random_fixed_freq_seq(n, rng)`:
```
x=np.arange(n)/n
a=np.ones(n)
for m in rng.choice(1..20, size=6, replace=False):
   ph=rng.uniform(0,2π); amp=rng.uniform(0,0.3,1)
   a += amp*(cos or sin)
clip,normalize
```

`interpolate_to(a, m)`:
```
x0=np.linspace(0,1,len(a)); x1=np.linspace(0,1,m); y=np.interp(x1,x0,a); return normalize(y)
```

Local long function:
`peak_backtracking(a0, budget_s, deadline, top_k=128, alphas=...)`
Pseudo:
```
def peak_backtracking(a0, deadline, max_seconds=600, top_k=128, verbose=False):
  a=normalize(np.maximum(a0,0))
  best=exact_score(a); best_a=a.copy()
  alpha=0.05*
  n=a.size
  while time.monotonic() + 5 < deadline:
     c=linear_conv(a); M=c.max(); S=1; sc=2*n*M
     if sc<best: ...
     # peaks
     if top_k >= c.size: idx=np.arange(c.size)
     else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:]
     # maybe include all near max
     g=np.zeros(n)
     for k in idx:
       k=int(k); s=max(0,k-(n-1)); e=min(k,n-1)
       if e>=s:
          # need values a[k-i] for i=s..e: slice a[k-e:k-s+1][::-1]
          g[s:e+1] += a[k-e:k-s+1][::-1]
     g /= len(idx)
     H = 2.0 * g
     H = H - np.mean(H)
     normH=np.linalg.norm(H)
     if normH<1e-14: break
     dir=-H/normH
     step_best=0; best_sc_local=sc; best_a_local=a.copy()
     # candidate alphas scale by norm_b
     norm_b=np.linalg.norm(a)
     for denom in [1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1]:
        step=0.1*denom*norm_b? maybe start 0.05*norm_b
        a_new=np.clip(a+step*dir,0.0,1000.0)
        S=a_new.sum(); if S<1e-12: continue
        a_new/=S
        sc_new=exact_score(a_new)
        if sc_new < best_sc_local: best_sc_local=sc_new; best_a_local=a_new; step_best=step
     if best_sc_local < sc: a=best_a_local; update
     else: # no step decrease? maybe alpha *=0.5? Could use random perturb
        # try smaller
        step=0.001*norm_b
        ...
        if no improve: break or randomize active subset
     # Adaptive top_k maybe increase as converge
  return best_a
```

def lse_normalized_objective(x, beta):
   n=x.size; S=x.sum(); if S<...; b=x/S; c=...; cm=c.max(); scaled=beta*(c-cm); ... L=...
   F=2*n*L

   X=np.fft.rfft(b,Lc); PR=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1],Lc); conv=np.fft.irfft(X*PR,Lc)
   idx=2*n-2 - np.arange(n)
   g=conv[idx].real

`polynomial_sqrt_shape` function:
```
def sqrt_shape_iteration(a0, n_iters, alpha=0.5, newton_per=1, mix=0.7, deadline=None):
   a=normalize(a0); n=a.size; M=2*n-1; L=fft_pow2(2*M-1) # ~4n
   for it in range(n_iters):
      if deadline and time... break
      c=linear_conv(a)
      med = np.median(c[c>0]) if any else c.max()
      c_safe=np.maximum(c,1e-300)
      med=max(med,1e-300)
      target = c_safe * (med/c_safe)**alpha
      target = target / target.sum()
      # Newton sqrt in cyclic length L
      A=np.fft.rfft(a, L) # a length n zero-padded to L? np.fft.rfft pads to L automatically.
      C=np.fft.rfft(target, L) # target length M zero-padded
      for _ in range(newton):
          A_safe=np.where(np.abs(A)>1e-12, A, 1e-12+0j)
          A = 0.5*(A + C/A_safe)
      a_t=np.fft.irfft(A, L)[:n]
      a_new=np.maximum((1-mix)*a + mix*a_t,0.0)
      S=a_new.sum()
      if S<1e-12: break
      a=normalize(a_new)
   return a
```
Will this help? It uses C/A where C/A frequency: if C has zeros, division large? Safe. But C/A might not converge if C/A singular; still a global move. Could produce negative large. Clip. We can include small mix to not destroy. This is experimental.

`make_fourier_basis(n, P)`:
```
def make_fourier_basis(n, P):
   x=np.arange(n, dtype=float)/n
   d=1+2*P
   B=np.empty((n,d), dtype=float)
   B[:,0]=1.0
   col=1
   for m in range(1,P+1):
      w=2*np.pi*m*x
      B[:,col]=np.cos(w)
      B[:,col+1]=np.sin(w)
      col+=2
   return B
```
Memory for n=8192,P=12: 8192*25=205k double ~1.6MB, fine.

`lowdim_objective(theta, n, B, beta):`

- `pen = 0.0`
- If `raw.min() < 0`: `pen += 1e6 * (min(0, raw.min())**2 + 1e-12)`? To prevent negative.
- `a = np.maximum(raw,0)`
- `S=a.sum()`; if S<1e-6 return 1e10+pen.
- `b=a/S`
- Compute smooth score: c=linear_conv(b); cm=c.max(); if cm<1e-300?; scaled=beta*(c-cm); ex=np.exp(scaled); Z=ex.sum(); L=cm+np.log(Z)/beta; F=2nL.
- return F+pen.
This uses exact conv each eval; for n=8192, beta irrelevant for gradient but for smooth.
But Powell may evaluate many times; n=8192 fine. To speed, use smaller n for Fourier search? If we optimize on n_work 6962, okay. Each conv 16384 FFT ~ maybe 0.003s; 10k eval=30s.

Now `peak_backtracking` details with no closures. Let's write a top-level function `peak_descent(a0, deadline, max_seconds=300, top_k=128, verbose=False)`. It returns best. Use local variable `t_exit = min(deadline, start + max_seconds)`.
Pseudo:
```
def peak_descent(a0, deadline, max_seconds=300, top_k=128, seed=0):
    a=normalize(np.maximum(a0,0.0))
    n=a.size
    best_sc=exact_score(a); best_a=a.copy()
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    exit_t=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+max_seconds)
    scale=0.2
    rng_top? 
    for it in range(100000):
       if time.monotonic()>=exit_t: break
       c=linear_conv(a); M=float(c.max()); sc=2*n*M
       if sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best_a=a.copy()
       # active peaks: choose top K, but randomize if stuck?
       if top_k < c.size:
          idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:]
       else: idx=np.arange(c.size)
       g=np.zeros(n)
       for k in idx:
          k=int(k)
          s=max(0,k-(n-1)); e=min(k,n-1)
          if e>=s:
             g[s:e+1] += a[k-e:k-s+1][::-1]
       g /= max(1,len(idx))
       H=2.0*g
       H -= np.mean(H)
       normH=np.linalg.norm(H)
       if normH < 1e-14: break
       direction = -H/normH
       norm_b=np.linalg.norm(a)
       improved=False
       # Try current scale, then halves
       for factor in (scale, 0.5*scale, 0.25*scale, 0.1*scale):
           step = factor * norm_b
           if step <= 0 or step > 1e-1: step= min(step,1e-1)
           a_new=np.clip(a + step*direction, 0.0, 1000.0)
           S=a_new.sum()
           if S<1e-12: continue
           a_new = a_new/S
           sc_new=exact_score(a_new)
           if sc_new < sc:  # or < best?
               a=a_new; sc=sc_new
               if sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best_a=a.copy()
               scale = min(1.0, factor*1.05) # keep or slightly increase
               improved=True
               break
       if not improved:
           scale *= 0.5
           if scale < 1e-4:
              # random restart within basin
              a = a + 0.01*norm_b*rng.standard_normal(n)
              a = normalize(np.maximum(a,0.0))
              scale=0.05
       # Optionally add random jitter to active? 
    return best_a
```
This uses exact_score each trial, 4 conv per iter. For 300s maybe ~5000 conv, okay.
Need choose top_k. For n=6962, top 128. If many peaks, okay. Could adapt: start top_k=64, increase as scale decreases? Maybe leave.

Potential bug: `a[k-e:k-s+1]` indexing. For k=1,n>1, s=0,e=1; slice a[1:2]? want for i=0..1 values a[1],a[0]. `k-e=0`, `k-s+1=2`, slice a[0:2]=a[0],a[1];[::-1]=a[1],a[0]. correct. For k=n-1 center, s=0,e=n-1, slice a[0:n], reverse. Good. For k=2n-3 last, s=n-2,e=n-1, slice a[2n-3-(n-1)=n-2 : 2n-3: +1? k-s+1= n-1? wait k=2n-3,s=n-2 => k-s+1=n? slice a[n-2:n] length2, values a[n-2],a[n-1] reversed = a[n-1],a[n-2]. L-BFGS function:

def lse_lbfgs_polish(a0, betas, deadline, max_seconds=300):
   a=normalize(a0)
   best_sc=exact_score(a); best_a=a.copy()
   t_exit=min(deadline, start+max_seconds)
   for beta in betas:
      if start_time > t_exit: break
      # maxiter based on remaining
      rem=t_exit-time.monotonic()
      if rem<30: break
      maxiter=min(2000, int(rem*10)) # maybe 10 iter/s? L-BFGS calls many eval.
      maxfun=maxiter*20
      res=minimize(lse_normalized_objective, a, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options=...)
      a_out=clip(res.x,0); normalize; sc=exact_score(a_out); update
   # maybe final exact peak after lbfgs
   return best_a
```

`fft_pow2(m)`: if m <=1 return1.
```
def fft_pow2(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()
```

`linear_conv(a)`: ensure a 1d.
```
def linear_conv(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    M=2*n-1
    L=fft_pow2(M)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:M]
    return c
```
If n=0? no.

`exact_score(a)` should handle list. If n huge and S <0.01? evaluate_sequence would inf, but internally sum scale arbitrary. We normalize candidates sum1; if not, score scale invariant but if S very small, numerical. Use if S<1e-12 return inf.

`normalize(a)`:
```
def normalize(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).copy()
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
   S=a.sum()
   if S<=1e-12:
       return np.full_like(a,1.0/max(1,a.size))
   return a/S
```
If a.size=0? no.

`b_sequence(n)`:
```
b=np.empty(n); b[0]=1.0
for i in range(1,n):
  b[i]=b[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
return normalize(b)
```
For n=1 okay.

return normalize(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
```

Now `sqrt_shape_iteration` maybe should be optional and not too time-consuming. It uses Newton division; could produce NaNs if C/A huge. Need safe:
```
def sqrt_shape_iteration(a0, n_iters=50, alpha=0.7, newton=1, mix=0.8, deadline=None, max_seconds=120):
   a=normalize(a0); start=time.monotonic(); exit_t=min(deadline if deadline else inf, start+max_seconds)
   n=a.size; M=2*n-1; L=fft_pow2(2*M-1) # if n large L 32768
   for _ in range(n_iters):
      if time.monotonic()>=exit_t: break
      c=linear_conv(a)
      pos=c[c>0]
      med=float(np.median(pos)) if pos.size else float(c.max())
      c_safe=np.maximum(c,1e-300)
      target = c_safe * np.power(med/c_safe, alpha)
      target = target / target.sum()
      A=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
      C=np.fft.rfft(target, L)
      for _ in range(newton):
          Am=np.abs(A)
          A_safe=np.where(Am>1e-12, A, (1e-12+0j))
          # if C has NaN? if not
          A = 0.5*(A + C/A_safe)
          # Clean tiny imaginary? A complex
      a_t=np.fft.irfft(A, L)[:n]
      a_new=(1-mix)*a + mix*a_t
      a_new=np.maximum(a_new,0.0)
      S=a_new.sum()
      if not np.isfinite(S) or S<1e-12: break
      a=normalize(a_new)
   return a
```
Could A diverge due C/A if C has large values where A zero. Use safe denominator and maybe blend A with previous. Since it's experimental; if it produces bad, we track best exact each iter? The function returns only final; better track best inside and return best. Need exact_score each iter expensive but okay.

Define top-level:
```
def add_to_pool(pool_scores, pool_arrays, score, a, pool_size=8):
    if not np.isfinite(score): return
    a=np.clip(np.asarray(a,float),0,1000)
    if a.sum() <=0: return
    if len(pool_scores) < pool_size:
        pool_scores.append(float(score)); pool_arrays.append(a.copy())
    else:
        worst=0
        for i in range(1,len(pool_scores)):
            if pool_scores[i] > pool_scores[worst]: worst=i
        if score < pool_scores[worst]:
            pool_scores[worst]=float(score); pool_arrays[worst]=a.copy()
```
But `best_score` still maintained in main when score < best.

      if pool_scores[i] < pool_scores[idx]: idx=i
   return pool_arrays[idx]
```

S = float(np.sum(x))
if S < 1e-8: return (1e10, np.zeros_like(x))
b = x / S
c = linear_conv(b)
n=b.size
cm=float(np.max(c))
scaled = beta*(c-cm)
# avoid overflow: scaled max0; if min<-745 underflows okay. ex=np.exp(np.maximum(scaled,-745))
ex = np.exp(scaled) # since max 0, underflow okay
Z=sum(ex)
if Z<=0: ...
L=cm + np.log(Z)/beta
F=2*n*L
p=ex/Z
M=2*n-1
Lc=fft_pow2(M+n-1)
X=np.fft.rfft(b, Lc)
PR=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], Lc)
conv=np.fft.irfft(X*PR, Lc)
idx = 2*n - 2 - np.arange(n)
g=conv[idx]
H=4.0*n*(g - np.mean(g))
grad=H/S
# replace nan/inf
grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
return float(F), grad
```

Now implement `random_initialization(n, rng)`:
```
def random_initialization(n, rng):
    i=np.arange(n)
    x=i/n
    a=np.ones(n)
    P=6
    for m in rng.integers(1, 20, size=P):
       amp=rng.uniform(-0.3,0.3)
       ph=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
       a += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*x + ph)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    return normalize(a)
```
Maybe add power.

`interpolate_sequence(a, m)`:
```
def interpolate_sequence(a, m):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
   n=a.size
   if n==m: return normalize(a)
   x0=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   x1=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
   y=np.interp(x1, x0, a)
   return normalize(y)
```

```
def get_top_indices(scores, k):
   idxs=list(range(len(scores)))
   # insertion sort
   for i in range(1,len(idxs)):
       x=idxs[i]; j=i-1
       while j>=0 and scores[idxs[j]] > scores[x]:
           idxs[j+1]=idxs[j]; j-=1
       idxs[j+1]=x
   return idxs[:k]
```
Top-level.

`fourier_search(n, Bwave, best_a, beta, maxfev)`:
```
def fourier_search(n, Bwave, best_a, beta, maxfev):
    d=Bwave.shape[1]
    if d==0: return None
    starts=[]
    # project best
    a=normalize(best_a)
    target = a * float(n) - 1.0
    try:
       theta = np.linalg.lstsq(Bwave, target, rcond=None)[0]
    except Exception:
       theta = np.zeros(d)
    theta = np.clip(theta, -1.2, 1.2)
    starts.append(theta)
    starts.append(np.zeros(d))

`lowdim_objective` uses `Bwave @ theta` and penalty for theta clipping? We'll implement:
```
def lowdim_objective(theta, n, Bwave, beta):
    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64)
    pen = 0.0
    over = np.maximum(0.0, np.abs(theta) - 1.2)
    if over.size:
       pen += 1e4 * float(over.sum())
    th = np.clip(theta, -1.2, 1.2)
    raw = 1.0 + Bwave.dot(th)
    vmin = float(np.min(raw))
    if vmin < 0.0:
       pen += 1e5 * vmin * vmin
       raw = np.maximum(raw, 0.0)
    S = float(np.sum(raw))
    if S <= 1e-8: return 1e12 + pen, zeros? Powell only fun.
    a=raw/S
    c=linear_conv(a)
    cm=float(np.max(c))
    scaled=beta*(c-cm)
    ex=np.exp(np.maximum(scaled, -745.0))
    Z=float(np.sum(ex))
    if Z <=0: return 1e12+pen
    L=cm + np.log(Z)/beta
    return float(2.0*n*L + pen)
```
No gradient needed.

`make_wave_basis(n,P)`:
```
def make_wave_basis(n,P):
    x=np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)/float(n)
    B=np.empty((n, 2*P), dtype=np.float64)
    c=0
    for m in range(1,P+1):
        w=2.0*np.pi*m*x
        B[:,c]=np.cos(w); B[:,c+1]=np.sin(w); c+=2
    return B
```
`make_sine_basis(n,P)` similarly with P columns sin. Ensure if P=0 shape (n,0).

Implement `update_best`:
```
def update_best(best_score, best_a, score, a, label=""):
    if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
        a2 = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        a2 = np.clip(a2, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if a2.sum() > 0:
            print(f"NEW BEST: {score:.8f} n={a2.size} label={label}")
            return score, a2, True
    return best_score, best_a, False
```

`get_top_indices` manual sort:
```
def get_top_indices(scores, k):
    idxs = list(range(len(scores)))
    for i in range(1, len(idxs)):
        cur = idxs[i]
        j = i - 1
        while j >= 0 and scores[idxs[j]] > scores[cur]:
            idxs[j+1] = idxs[j]
            j -= 1
        idxs[j+1] = cur
    return idxs[:k]
```
Pool scores finite. If k > len, returns all.

`pool_best`: use get_top_indices? Or loop.
```
def pool_best(pool_scores, pool_arrays):
   if not pool_scores: return None
   best_idx=0
   for i in range(1,len(pool_scores)):
      if pool_scores[i] < pool_scores[best_idx]: best_idx=i
   return pool_arrays[best_idx]
```

```python
"""
Multistage search for a non-negative step function minimizing
    2*n*max(self-convolution) / (sum(sequence))^2.

The program combines:
  - a parent sequence if one is available,
  - analytic initializations (one-sided singular / power-law templates),
  - low-dimensional Fourier-subspace derivative-free optimization,
  - a square-root / target-convolution spectral iteration,
  - active-peak projected subgradient descent with exact backtracking,
  - L-BFGS-B polishing of a scale-invariant log-sum-exp smoothed objective.

All convolution-based internal scoring is FFT-based.  The external
evaluate_sequence() is only called once at the end, to verify the returned
list.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize


def fft_pow2(m):
    """Next power of two >= m (at least 1)."""
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def linear_conv(a):
    """Full linear convolution a*a via FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    m = 2 * n - 1
    L = fft_pow2(m)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    return np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[:m]


def normalize(a):
    """Clip non-negative and normalize to sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-12:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / max(1, a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a / S


def exact_score(a):
    """Internal objective, scale invariant."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    c = linear_conv(a)
    m = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if m < 0.0 or not np.isfinite(m):
        m = 0.0
    n = a.size
    return float(2.0 * n * m) / (S * S)


def update_best(best_score, best_a, score, a, label=""):
    """Return updated (best_score, best_a) if score improves."""
    if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
        a2 = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        a2 = np.nan_to_num(a2, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
        a2 = np.clip(a2, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if a2.sum() > 0.0:
            print("NEW BEST: %.10f  n=%d  label=%s" % (score, a2.size, label))
            return float(score), a2
    return best_score, best_a


def add_to_pool(pool_scores, pool_arrays, score, a, pool_size=12):
    """Maintain a small pool of good candidates of arbitrary lengths."""
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if a.sum() <= 0.0:
        return
    if len(pool_scores) < pool_size:
        pool_scores.append(float(score))
        pool_arrays.append(a.copy())
        return
    worst = 0
    for i in range(1, len(pool_scores)):
        if pool_scores[i] > pool_scores[worst]:
            worst = i
    if score < pool_scores[worst]:
        pool_scores[worst] = float(score)
        pool_arrays[worst] = a.copy()


def pool_best(pool_scores, pool_arrays):
    if not pool_scores:
        return None
    bi = 0
    for i in range(1, len(pool_scores)):
        if pool_scores[i] < pool_scores[bi]:
            bi = i
    return pool_arrays[bi]


def get_top_indices(scores, k):
    """Top k indices by increasing score, small manual sort (no lambda)."""
    idxs = list(range(len(scores)))
    for i in range(1, len(idxs)):
        cur = idxs[i]
        j = i - 1
        while j >= 0 and scores[idxs[j]] > scores[cur]:
            idxs[j + 1] = idxs[j]
            j -= 1
        idxs[j + 1] = cur
    if k < len(idxs):
        return idxs[:k]
    return idxs


def b_sequence(n):
    """
    Truncated coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2):
    b_k = C(2k,k)/4^k.  This gives left-half convolution exactly constant
    (unnormalized) and is a strong one-sided singular-template baseline.
    """
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1)
    b = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        b[i] = b[i - 1] * (2 * i - 1) / (2.0 * i)
    return normalize(b)


def power_sequence(n, p, right_weight=0.0):
    """Monotone power-law template, optionally with a right-sided tail."""
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.power(i + 1.0, -p)
    if right_weight > 0.0:
        j = n - i
        a = a + right_weight * np.power(j + 1.0, -p)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    return normalize(a)


def two_level_sequence(n, ratio, blocks=2):
    """Two/multi-level block template.  Useful for exploration."""
    blocks = max(2, int(blocks))
    a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    chunk = max(1, n // blocks)
    for b in range(blocks):
        start = b * chunk
        stop = (b + 1) * chunk if b + 1 < blocks else n
        if b % 2 == 0:
            a[start:stop] *= ratio
        else:
            a[start:stop] *= 1.0 / max(1e-12, ratio)
    return normalize(a)


def random_initialization(n, rng):
    """Random low-frequency non-negative pattern."""
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    P = 7
    used = set()
    for _ in range(P):
        m = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n // 4)))
        if m in used:
            continue
        used.add(m)
        amp = float(rng.uniform(-0.28, 0.28))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        a += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * x + ph)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    return normalize(a)


def interpolate_sequence(a, m):
    """Resample a normalized sequence to length m while preserving sum ~1."""
    a = normalize(a)
    n = a.size
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return np.arange(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == n:
        return a
    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    y = np.interp(x1, x0, a)
    return normalize(y)


def make_wave_basis(n, P):
    """Fourier cosine/sine basis up to frequency P."""
    P = int(max(1, P))
    n = int(n)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    B = np.empty((n, 2 * P), dtype=np.float64)
    col = 0
    for m in range(1, P + 1):
        w = 2.0 * np.pi * m * x
        B[:, col] = np.cos(w)
        B[:, col + 1] = np.sin(w)
        col += 2
    return B


def make_sine_basis(n, P):
    """Sine-only basis; sine modes are antisymmetric about the center."""
    P = int(max(1, P))
    n = int(n)
    if P <= 0:
        return np.empty((n, 0), dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    B = np.empty((n, P), dtype=np.float64)
    for j, m in enumerate(range(1, P + 1)):
        B[:, j] = np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * x)
    return B


def fourier_to_sequence(n, Bwave, theta):
    """Convert Fourier parameters into a normalized non-negative sequence."""
    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    theta = np.clip(theta, -1.2, 1.2)
    raw = 1.0 + Bwave.dot(theta)
    a = np.maximum(raw, 0.0)
    return normalize(a)


def lowdim_objective(theta, n, Bwave, beta):
    """Objective for Fourier-subspace search; soft-penalizes negativity."""
    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    over = np.maximum(0.0, np.abs(theta) - 1.2)
    pen = 1e4 * float(over.sum())
    theta = np.clip(theta, -1.2, 1.2)
    raw = 1.0 + Bwave.dot(theta)
    vmin = float(np.min(raw))
    if vmin < 0.0:
        pen += 1e5 * vmin * vmin
        raw = np.maximum(raw, 0.0)
    S = float(np.sum(raw))
    if S <= 1e-8:
        return 1e12 + pen
    a = raw / S
    c = linear_conv(a)
    cm = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    scaled = beta * (c - cm)
    scaled = np.maximum(scaled, -745.0)
    ex = np.exp(scaled)
    Z = float(np.sum(ex))
    if Z <= 0.0:
        return 1e12 + pen
    L = cm + np.log(Z) / float(beta)
    return float(2.0 * n * L + pen)


def fourier_search(n, Bwave, best_a, beta, maxfev, max_seconds=240.0):
    """Derivative-free search in a low-dimensional Fourier subspace."""
    Bwave = np.asarray(Bwave, dtype=np.float64)
    d = Bwave.shape[1]
    if d <= 0:
        return None

    starts = []
    a_ref = normalize(best_a)
    target = a_ref * float(n) - 1.0
    try:
        th0 = np.linalg.lstsq(Bwave, target, rcond=None)[0].ravel()
        starts.append(np.clip(th0, -1.2, 1.2))
    except Exception:
        starts.append(np.zeros(d, dtype=np.float64))

    starts.append(np.zeros(d, dtype=np.float64))
    r1 = np.random.default_rng(101)
    r2 = np.random.default_rng(202)
    starts.append(np.clip(0.12 * r1.standard_normal(d), -1.2, 1.2))
    starts.append(np.clip(0.30 * r2.standard_normal(d), -1.2, 1.2))

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    exit_t = t0 + max(0.0, float(max_seconds))
    best_local = None
    best_sc = np.inf

    for th in starts:
        if time.monotonic() >= exit_t:
            break
        cand = fourier_to_sequence(n, Bwave, th)
        sc = exact_score(cand)
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best_local = cand.copy()

        try:
            res = minimize(
                lowdim_objective,
                th.copy(),
                args=(n, Bwave, beta),
                method="Powell",
                options={
                    "maxfev": int(max(50, maxfev)),
                    "xtol": 1e-8,
                    "ftol": 1e-10,
                }
            )
            thf = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            cand = fourier_to_sequence(n, Bwave, thf)
            sc = exact_score(cand)
            if sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best_local = cand.copy()
        except Exception:
            pass

    return best_local


def sqrt_shape_iteration(a0, n_iters, alpha, newton, mix, max_seconds=90.0):
    """
    Spectral square-root projection toward a flattened target convolution.
    This is a different experimental move from ordinary Fienup: it targets
    c = a*a rather than |A|^2.
    """
    a = normalize(a0)
    n = a.size
    m = 2 * n - 1
    L = fft_pow2(2 * m - 1)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    exit_t = t0 + max(0.0, float(max_seconds))

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score(a)

    for _ in range(int(max(1, n_iters))):
        if time.monotonic() >= exit_t:
            break
        c = linear_conv(a)
        pos = c[c > 1e-300]
        med = float(np.median(pos)) if pos.size else float(np.max(c))
        med = max(med, 1e-300)
        c_safe = np.maximum(c, 1e-300)
        target = c_safe * np.power(med / c_safe, float(alpha))
        ts = float(target.sum())
        if ts <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ts):
            break
        target = target / ts

        try:
            A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
            C = np.fft.rfft(target, L)
            for _ in range(int(max(1, newton))):
                A_safe = np.where(np.abs(A) > 1e-12, A, (1e-12 + 0.0j))
                A = 0.5 * (A + C / A_safe)
                if not np.all(np.isfinite(A)):
                    raise ArithmeticError
            a_t = np.fft.irfft(A, L)[:n]
            a_new = (1.0 - float(mix)) * a + float(mix) * a_t
            a_new = np.maximum(a_new, 0.0)
            S = float(a_new.sum())
            if S <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(S):
                break
            a = a_new / S
            sc = exact_score(a)
            if sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best_a = a.copy()
        except Exception:
            break

    return best_a


def peak_descent(a0, deadline, max_seconds, top_k=128, seed=0):
    """
    Active-peak projected subgradient descent with exact backtracking.
    For selected peak lags k, the subgradient of max(a*a) is represented by
    averages of shifted a, projected to preserve the simplex sum.
    """
    a = normalize(a0)
    n = a.size
    top_k = int(max(1, min(top_k, 2 * n - 1)))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    exit_t = min(float(deadline), time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(max_seconds)))

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score(a)

    scale = 0.08
    stagnate = 0
    iter_count = 0

    while time.monotonic() < exit_t:
        iter_count += 1
        c = linear_conv(a)
        M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        sc = float(2.0 * n * M)
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best_a = a.copy()

        if top_k >= c.size:
            idx = np.arange(c.size)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:]

        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for kk in idx:
            kk = int(kk)
            s = max(0, kk - n + 1)
            e = min(kk, n - 1)
            if e >= s:
                g[s:e + 1] += a[kk - e:kk - s + 1][::-1]
        g /= float(max(1, idx.size))

        H = 2.0 * g
        H -= np.mean(H)
        normH = float(np.linalg.norm(H))
        if normH < 1e-15:
            break

        direction = -H / normH
        norm_b = float(np.linalg.norm(a))
        improved = False

        for factor in (scale, 0.5 * scale, 0.25 * scale, 0.1 * scale):
            step = factor * norm_b
            if step <= 0.0:
                continue
            step = min(step, 0.05)
            a_new = np.clip(a + step * direction, 0.0, 1000.0)
            S = float(a_new.sum())
            if S <= 1e-12:
                continue
            a_new = a_new / S
            sc_new = exact_score(a_new)
            if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < sc:
                a = a_new
                sc = sc_new
                if sc < best_sc:
                    best_sc = sc
                    best_a = a.copy()
                scale = min(0.25, factor * 1.1 + 1e-6)
                improved = True
                break

        if not improved:
            stagnate += 1
            scale *= 0.5
            if scale < 1e-4 or stagnate > 80:
                jitter = 0.02 * norm_b
                a = np.clip(a + jitter * rng.standard_normal(n), 0.0, 1000.0)
                S = float(a.sum())
                if S <= 1e-12:
                    break
                a = a / S
                scale = 0.03
                stagnate = 0
        else:
            stagnate = 0

        if iter_count % 500 == 0 and time.monotonic() < exit_t:
            print("  peak_descent iteration=%d score=%.10f" % (iter_count, best_sc))

    return best_a


def lse_normalized_objective(x, beta):
    """
    Scale-invariant smoothed objective for L-BFGS:
       minimize 2*n*logsumexp(beta*c)/beta
    with c = conv(x_normalized, x_normalized).
    The gradient is projected to sum-zero so scaling is neutral.
    """
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
    S = float(x.sum())
    if S < 1e-8:
        return 1e10, np.zeros_like(x)

    b = x / S
    n = b.size
    c = linear_conv(b)
    cm = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0

    scaled = float(beta) * (c - cm)
    scaled = np.maximum(scaled, -745.0)
    ex = np.exp(scaled)
    Z = float(np.sum(ex))
    if Z <= 0.0:
        return 1e10, np.zeros_like(x)

    L = cm + np.log(Z) / float(beta)
    F = float(2.0 * n * L)
    p = ex / Z

    m = 2 * n - 1
    Lc = fft_pow2(m + n - 1)
    X = np.fft.rfft(b, Lc)
    PR = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], Lc)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(X * PR, Lc)
    idx = 2 * n - 2 - np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    g = conv[idx]

    H = 4.0 * float(n) * (g - float(np.mean(g)))
    grad = H / S
    grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    return F, grad


def lse_lbfgs_polish(a0, betas, deadline, max_seconds=240.0):
    """L-BFGS-B polishing on the scale-invariant LSE objective."""
    a = normalize(a0)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score(a)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    exit_t = min(float(deadline), t0 + max(0.0, float(max_seconds)))

    n = a.size
    bounds = [(0.0, 100.0)] * n

    for beta in betas:
        if time.monotonic() >= exit_t:
            break
        rem = exit_t - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 25.0:
            break
        maxfun = int(max(200, min(1500, rem * 12.0)))
        maxiter = int(max(40, min(500, maxfun // 5)))
        try:
            res = minimize(
                lse_normalized_objective,
                a.copy(),
                args=(float(beta),),
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                jac=True,
                bounds=bounds,
                options={
                    "maxiter": maxiter,
                    "maxfun": maxfun,
                    "ftol": 1e-13,
                    "gtol": 1e-8,
                    "maxls": 30,
                }
            )
            a_out = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            a_out = np.clip(a_out, 0.0, 1000.0)
            a_out = normalize(a_out)
            sc = exact_score(a_out)
            if sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best_a = a_out.copy()
                a = a_out.copy()
        except Exception:
            break

    return best_a


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """Main search entry point."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = float(budget_s)
    deadline = t0 + max(120.0, total_budget - 60.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    parent = None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if p.size >= 64:
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if p.sum() > 0.0:
                parent = p / p.sum()
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    pool_scores = []
    pool_arrays = []
    best_score = np.inf
    best_a = None

    def cand_fn(a, label):
        nonlocal best_score, best_a
        if a is None:
            return
        a = normalize(a)
        sc = exact_score(a)
        best_score, best_a = update_best(best_score, best_a, sc, a, label)
        add_to_pool(pool_scores, pool_arrays, sc, a)

    print("Budget: %.1fs, deadline in %.1fs" % (total_budget, deadline - t0))
    print("Parent available: %s" % (parent is not None))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Initial candidates.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    cand_fn(parent, "parent")

    if parent is not None:
        base_n = parent.size
    else:
        base_n = 8192
    if base_n < 2048:
        base_n = 4096

    base_n = int(base_n)
    cand_fn(b_sequence(base_n), "B-left")
    cand_fn(b_sequence(base_n)[::-1], "B-right")
    cand_fn(power_sequence(base_n, 0.45), "pow045")
    cand_fn(power_sequence(base_n, 0.55, right_weight=0.25), "pow055R")
    cand_fn(two_level_sequence(base_n, 1.5, blocks=2), "2lvl15")
    cand_fn(two_level_sequence(base_n, 0.7, blocks=3), "3lvl07")
    for i in range(4):
        cand_fn(random_initialization(base_n, rng), "rand%02d" % i)

    # A different length is often useful; 8192 is a good FFT-friendly size.
    alt_n = 8192 if best_a is None or best_a.size != 8192 else 4096
    if best_a is not None:
        cand_fn(interpolate_sequence(best_a, alt_n), "interpolated-alt")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Low-dimensional Fourier-subspace search on the current focus length.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    focus = pool_best(pool_scores, pool_arrays)
    if focus is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 420:
        nf = focus.size
        P = 6 if nf > 6000 else 8
        Bw = make_wave_basis(nf, P)
        maxfev = 360 if nf > 6000 else 800
        mx = 150.0 if nf > 6000 else 220.0
        cand = fourier_search(nf, Bw, focus, 220, maxfev, max_seconds=mx)
        cand_fn(cand, "fourier-wave")

        Ps = 5 if nf > 6000 else 7
        Bs = make_sine_basis(nf, Ps)
        cand = fourier_search(nf, Bs, focus, 220, 320 if nf > 6000 else 700,
                              max_seconds=110.0)
        cand_fn(cand, "fourier-sine")

        # Mix the Fourier search result with the focus sequence.
        cur_best = pool_best(pool_scores, pool_arrays)
        if cand is not None and cur_best is not None and cand.size == cur_best.size:
            for tt in (0.5, 0.7, 0.3):
                cand_fn(normalize(tt * cand + (1.0 - tt) * cur_best),
                        "mix-fourier-%.1f" % tt)

    # Small random perturbations of the current best.
    cur_best = pool_best(pool_scores, pool_arrays)
    if cur_best is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 400:
        for noise in (0.015, 0.035, 0.07):
            jitter = normalize(np.maximum(
                cur_best * (1.0 + noise * rng.standard_normal(cur_best.size)), 0.0))
            cand_fn(jitter, "noise-%.3f" % noise)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Square-root target-convolution spectral diversification.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 350:
        top = get_top_indices(pool_scores, 2)
        for idx in top:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 300:
                break
            cand = sqrt_shape_iteration(
                pool_arrays[idx],
                n_iters=40,
                alpha=0.65,
                newton=1,
                mix=0.75,
                max_seconds=80.0
            )
            cand_fn(cand, "sqrt-spectral")

    # Rebuild focus list.
    top_focus = get_top_indices(pool_scores, 3)
    focus_arrays = [pool_arrays[i] for i in top_focus]

    # Ensure an alternate length is represented in focused local search.
    focus_has_alt = False
    for fa in focus_arrays:
        if fa.size == alt_n:
            focus_has_alt = True
            break
    if not focus_has_alt and cur_best is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 250:
        alt = interpolate_sequence(cur_best, alt_n)
        cand_fn(alt, "add-alt-focus")
        focus_arrays.append(alt)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Active-peak projected descent on top focused candidates.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    lbfgs_reserve = min(520.0, 0.32 * max(100.0, remaining))
    phase3_end = deadline - lbfgs_reserve
    print("Phase peak_descent until t=%.1f (remaining now %.1f)" %
          (phase3_end - t0, phase3_end - time.monotonic()))

    for ii, fa in enumerate(focus_arrays[:4]):
        if time.monotonic() >= phase3_end:
            break
        rem = phase3_end - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 35.0:
            break
        rem_per = rem / max(1, len(focus_arrays[:4]) - ii)
        maxsec = min(380.0, rem_per * 1.25)
        if maxsec < 20.0:
            break
        top_k = 160 if fa.size > 8000 else 128
        cand = peak_descent(
            fa,
            deadline=phase3_end,
            max_seconds=maxsec,
            top_k=top_k,
            seed=777 + 17 * ii
        )
        cand_fn(cand, "peak-%d" % ii)

    # One more peak pass on the absolute best if time remains.
    cur_best = pool_best(pool_scores, pool_arrays)
    if cur_best is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 200:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic() - 200
        if rem > 60:
            cand = peak_descent(cur_best, deadline, max(60, rem * 0.5),
                                top_k=200, seed=4242)
            cand_fn(cand, "peak-best")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # L-BFGS polishing on top candidates.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    print("Phase L-BFGS start at t=%.1f" % (time.monotonic() - t0))
    top_for_lbfgs = get_top_indices(pool_scores, 2)
    for ii, idx in enumerate(top_for_lbfgs):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 90:
            break
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic() - 90
        if rem < 60:
            break
        maxsec = min(rem, 260.0 - 30.0 * ii)
        if maxsec < 40:
            continue
        betas = (120, 500, 1800, 6000)
        cand = lse_lbfgs_polish(pool_arrays[idx], betas, deadline,
                                max_seconds=maxsec)
        cand_fn(cand, "lbfgs-%d" % ii)

    # Final high-beta polish of the absolute best.
    cur_best = pool_best(pool_scores, pool_arrays)
    if cur_best is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 70:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic() - 70
        if rem > 50:
            cand = lse_lbfgs_polish(cur_best, (1500, 4000, 10000), deadline,
                                    max_seconds=min(130.0, rem * 0.7))
            cand_fn(cand, "lbfgs-final-high")

    # Final short exact peak polish.
    cur_best = pool_best(pool_scores, pool_arrays)
    if cur_best is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 35:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic() - 35
        if rem > 35:
            cand = peak_descent(cur_best, deadline, min(70.0, rem * 0.9),
                                top_k=250, seed=9090)
            cand_fn(cand, "peak-final")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Fallback and return.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if best_a is None:
        best_a = b_sequence(4096)
        best_score = exact_score(best_a)

    out_a = np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    out_a = np.nan_to_num(out_a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    out_a = np.clip(out_a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(out_a.sum())
    if S < 0.01:
        out_a = np.full(4096, 0.5, dtype=np.float64)
        S = float(out_a.sum())
    out_a = out_a / S

    out = [float(x) for x in out_a]
    try:
        verified = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
    except Exception:
        verified = np.inf

    print("=" * 70)
    print("Internal best: %.10f" % best_score)
    print("Verified best: %.10f" % verified)
    print("Returned length: %d" % len(out))
    print("Total runtime: %.1fs" % (time.monotonic() - t0))
    print("=" * 70)
    return out
```
